Let me understand the task. We need to prove strong normalization for STLC with CBV semantics, locally nameless.

The typical approach: define a unary logical relation / reducibility predicate. For CBV STLC with if-then-else, we need:

- `SN t` := strongly_normalizing t (coinductive/inductive as given). Note `strongly_normalizing` is an Inductive with constructor `SN_intro : (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> SN t`. This is essentially the "all reducts SN" inductive definition (like Acc). We can use well-founded induction on it.

- Define `R T t` for closed terms:

Let's define:
```
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.
```

And `open` stays as defined.

Same. Good.

**Alternative strategy: use a "neutral" predicate à la Girard/Tait.** In Girard's terminology, for CBV:
- `neutral t` := t is not a value (or "t is stuck-headed": bvar, fvar, app, if).
- Reducibility candidates: 
  - R Bool t := lc t /\ SN t /\ (t -->* true ∨ t -->* false)
  - R (T1->T2) t := lc t /\ SN t /\ forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u)  (or the closure-based version)
  
and the "CR" lemmas: R is closed under reduction (if R T t and t --> t' then R T t'), and "R T t iff SN t and all ... ". 

The standard trick for applying a relation to a term that reduces: prove **R_closed_under_step**: `R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'`. For the arrow case, this needs SN t --> SN t' (easy: from SN t and step, get SN t') and forall u, R T2 (app t' u) from R T2 (app t u) and `app t u --> app t' u` — using ST_App1 with lc u. 5. R_sn, R_lc : projections.
6. Fundamental theorem.
7. Regularity (typing → lc). Needed before the fundamental theorem (for the abs case lc).
8. Final theorem.

```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.
```

```coq
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.
```

```coq
Lemma step_app_abs_value_cases : forall A body u Y,
  tm_app (tm_abs A body) u --> Y ->
  (value u /\ Y = open body u) \/
  (exists u', u --> u' /\ Y = tm_app (tm_abs A body) u').

  intros A body u Y H. inversion H; subst; clear H.
```

```coq
  strongly_normalizing t1 ->

Qed.
```

Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write it:

```coq
Definition env_match (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x).

Lemma env_update : forall Gamma sigma x u T,
  env_match Gamma sigma ->
  R T u ->
  env_match (update Gamma x T) (subst_update sigma x u).
Proof.
  intros Gamma sigma x u T Henv Hu y T' Hy.
  unfold update in Hy. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
  - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst. injection Hy as Hy. subst. 
    unfold subst_update. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. exact Hu.
  - unfold subst_update. rewrite E. apply Henv. exact Hy.
Qed.
```
Careful: `update Gamma x T y = if Nat.eqb x y then Some T else Gamma y`. ✓. And subst_update sigma x u y = if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y ✓. Since both use Nat.eqb x y, the same E applies ✓.

Hmm, `injection Hy as Hy` — Hy : Some T = Some T'; injection gives T = T' ✓.

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T H.
  assert (Hr : R T (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)).
  { apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty) (sigma := fun x => tm_fvar x).
    - exact H.
    - intros x T' Hx. unfold empty in Hx. discriminate. }
  rewrite subst_id in Hr. apply R_sn with T. exact Hr.
Qed.
```

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions for the normalization proof *)

(* Simultaneous substitution for free variables *)
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Definition subst_update (sigma : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else sigma y.

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.
```

```coq
Lemma subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof. induction t; simpl; f_equal; auto. Qed.
```

Let me be careful: `induction t; simpl; f_equal; auto` might leave goals where f_equal doesn't apply... Let me write `induction t; simpl; try reflexivity; try (f_equal; auto)`. Hmm, in the bvar case: subst id (bvar n) = bvar n ✓ reflexivity. fvar: subst id (fvar a) = (fun x => fvar x) a = fvar a ✓ (simpl reduces). abs: tm_abs T (subst id t) = tm_abs T t by f_equal + IH ✓. Let me just use `induction t; simpl; f_equal; auto` — for leaf cases, `f_equal` on `bvar n = bvar n`... f_equal would leave `n = n` which auto solves ✓. Actually `reflexivity` is simpler. Let me use a robust tactic: `induction t; simpl; try reflexivity; try (f_equal; auto)`.

```coq
(* ---------- Basic properties of the step relation and SN ---------- *)

Lemma step_abs_false : forall T body t', ~ (tm_abs T body --> t').
Proof. intros T body t' H; inversion H. Qed.

Lemma SN_intro' : forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t') -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros; apply SN_intro; auto. Qed.
```

Actually SN_intro is directly usable.

```coq
Lemma SN_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof. intros t t' H Hstep. destruct H as [t0 Hred]. apply Hred. exact Hstep. Qed.
```
Hmm, `destruct H` on `strongly_normalizing t` where t is a variable: gives Hred : forall t', t --> t' -> strongly_normalizing t' ✓. But the destruct will substitute t0 := t? Let me write `destruct H as [t0 Hred]` — hmm, the constructor is SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> SN t. When destructing H : SN t, Coq gives Hred : forall t', t --> t' -> SN t' ✓ (t is fixed).

```coq
Lemma SN_no_step : forall t, (forall t', ~ (t --> t')) -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros t H. apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep. exfalso. apply (H t'). exact Hstep. Qed.

Lemma SN_value_abs : forall T body, strongly_normalizing (tm_abs T body).
Proof. intros. apply SN_no_step. intros. apply step_abs_false. Qed.
```
Hmm, SN_no_step requires `forall t', ~ (t --> t')`; and step_abs_false is `forall T body t', ~ (tm_abs T body --> t')` ✓.

```coq
Lemma SN_true : strongly_normalizing tm_true.
```

```coq
Lemma lc_step : forall t t', t --> t' -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' H. induction H; intros Hlc.
  - (* ST_AppAbs: t = app (abs T t0) v, t' = open t0 v *)
    destruct (lc_at_app_inv 0 _ _ Hlc) as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_at_open_rec with (k := 0).
    + apply lc_at_abs_inv in H1. exact H1.
    + exact H2.
  - (* ST_App1: t = app t1 t2, t' = app t1' t2 *)
    destruct (lc_at_app_inv 0 _ _ Hlc) as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_app; [apply IHH | exact H2].  (* IHH : locally_closed t1 -> locally_closed t1' *)
  - (* ST_App2 *)
    destruct (lc_at_app_inv 0 _ _ Hlc) as [H1 H2].
    apply lc_app; [exact H1 | apply IHH; exact H2].
  - (* ST_IfTrue: t = if true t1 t2, t' = t1 *)
    destruct (lc_at_if_inv 0 _ _ _ Hlc) as [H1 [H2 H3]]. exact H2.
  - (* ST_IfFalse *) ... exact H3.
  - (* ST_If: t = if t1 t2 t3, t' = if t1' t2 t3 *)
    destruct (lc_at_if_inv 0 _ _ _ Hlc) as [H1 [H2 H3]].
    apply lc_if; [apply IHH; exact H1 | exact H2 | exact H3].
Qed.
```
Note: in ST_IfTrue, the rule is `tm_if tm_true t1 t2 --> t1`; lc_at 0 (tm_if tm_true t1 t2) gives lc_at 0 tm_true, lc_at 0 t1, lc_at 0 t2. t' = t1 ✓ so exact H2 ✓.

```coq
(* ---------- The logical relation ---------- *)

Fixpoint R (T : ty) : tm -> Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => fun t => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => fun t =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.

Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof. intros T; destruct T; simpl; intros t [H _]; exact H. Qed.

Lemma R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros T; destruct T; simpl; intros t [H [Hsn _]]; exact Hsn.
Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma fresh_not_in : forall L : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L.

  intros L. exists (S (fold_right Nat.add 0 L)).

Qed.
```

```coq
Lemma in_le_sum : forall (L : list atom) (x : atom), In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.add 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L IH]; simpl; intros x Hx.
  - inversion Hx.
  - destruct Hx as [-> | Hx]; lia.
Qed.

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall L : list atom, exists x, ~ In x L.
Proof.
  intros L. exists (S (fold_right Nat.add 0 L)).
  intro Hc. apply in_le_sum in Hc. lia.
Qed.
```

Then:
```coq
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H.
  - apply lc_fvar.
  - (* T_Abs *)
    destruct (fresh_not_in (L ++ fv t1)) as [x Hx].
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L) by (intro Hc; apply Hx; apply in_app_iff; left; exact Hc).
    assert (Hxl : ~ In x (fv t1)) by (intro Hc; apply Hx; apply in_app_iff; right; exact Hc).
    apply lc_abs. apply lc_at_open_fvar with (x := x).
    apply IHH with (x := x) (H := HxL).  (* naming *)
```

```coq
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
```

```coq
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
```

```coq
Definition env_match (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
```

Final theorem:
```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T H.
  assert (Hr : R T (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)).
  { apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty) (sigma := fun x => tm_fvar x).
    - exact H.
    - split.
      + intros x. apply lc_fvar.
      + intros x T' Hc. unfold empty in Hc. discriminate. }
  rewrite subst_id in Hr.
  apply R_sn with T. exact Hr.
Qed.
```
Careful: `fundamental`'s statement order: `forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> forall sigma, env_match Gamma sigma -> R T (subst sigma t)`. So `apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty) (sigma := ...)` — but sigma is introduced after H in the statement; `apply fundamental with` might work if Coq can instantiate the forall-bound variables. Since the goal is `R T (subst (fun x => fvar x) t)`, applying fundamental requires unifying `R T (subst sigma t)` with the goal → sigma := fun x => fvar x, T := T, t := t ✓. Then the remaining goals: `has_type empty t T` ✓ and `env_match empty sigma` ✓.

Let me write it as:
```
  { apply (fundamental empty t T H (fun x => tm_fvar x)).
    split; [intros x; apply lc_fvar | intros x T' Hc; unfold empty in Hc; discriminate]. }
```

```coq
Lemma lc_at_subst : forall sigma k t,
```

```coq
  induction Ht as [k i Hi | k x | k a b Ha IHa Hb IHb | k T a Ha IHa | k | k | k a b c Ha IHa Hb IHb Hc IHc];

  - apply lc_at_mono with (k := 0); [lia | apply Hsig].
  - apply lc_app; auto.
  - apply lc_abs. apply IHa. exact Hsig.
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - apply lc_if; auto.
Qed.
```
